  return 14;
} else {

}
}

import os
from dotenv import load_dotenv
from langchain_openai import OpenAI
from langchain_core.runnables import RunnableLambda
from langchain_community.runnables import ReasoningRunner
# load environment
load_dotenv()
# set up OpenAI client
client = OpenAI(api_key=os.getenv("OPENAI_API_KEY"))
# define the reasoning task

# build the chain
chain = reasoning_task.with_runnable(client, name="OpenAI/ChatCompletion")
# prepare the prompt

You are an expert in geospatial topological reasoning.

Vernacular-to-Topology Reference:
  WITHIN    — "is in", "located in", "part of"              (A is fully inside B)
  CONTAINS  — "is home to", "includes", "hosts"             (A fully encloses B)
  TOUCHES   — "borders", "adjacent to", "enclave of", "surrounded by", "crosses" (partial topological relation)
  CROSSES   — "passes through", "along", "traverses", "at the mouth of", "upstream from" (full LineString topological relation)
  OVERLAPS  — "partly in", "extends into", "straddles", "part of population in", "county seat of" (may extend into adjacent counties)
  DISJOINT  — "far from", "miles away", "separate from" (confirmed large distance)

WARNING — ambiguous vernacular (read carefully):
  "county seat of" → does NOT imply within — the city may extend into adjacent counties → overlaps
  "suburb of"      → does NOT imply touches — the city may be separated from the main city → disjoint
  "borders"        → maps to the topological predicate "touches" (geometries share a boundary edge).
  "at the mouth of" / "upstream from" (with a LineString) → likely crosses (full LineString traversal), not just touches.


Rules:
1. DIRECTION CHECK (mandatory before answering):
   — "borders" means A is the LARGER entity that SURROUNDS B; the topological relation is "touches" (A touches B).
   — Ask: does this predicate apply A→B or B→A? The answer is "touches" for A→B.

2. TEXTUAL QUALIFIER PRIORITY (override geometric signals when contradictory):
   — "borders" → touches (shared boundary edge).
   — "suburb of" → does NOT imply touches, treat as disjoint if no topological evidence.
   — "enclave of" → touches (shared boundary, not interior).
   — "at the mouth of" / "upstream from" (with a LineString) → likely crosses (geometries topologically related by crosses).

3. GEOMETRY TYPE CONSTRAINTS:
   — For a town (Polygon) and another town (Polygon), the topological relation for the vernacular "borders" is "touches".
   — No need to check the actual OSM geometry dimensions; the vernacular mapping is definitive.

4. BOUNDING BOX CAUTION (never conclude directly from bbox):
   — A bbox overlap for two towns does NOT prove a topological relation like overlaps or disjoint.
   — Treat bbox as a clue, never as proof.

5. BBOX GAP TOLERANCE:
   — A gap of < 0.01 degrees between bboxes is NOT proof of disjoint.
   — Administrative boundaries often share exact edges with 0-meter gap.

6. ENTITY VALIDATION:

   — You do not need to analyze the actual OSM geometry dimensions.

CRITICAL DISTINCTIONS (most confused predicates):

touches vs disjoint:
  touches  → A and B share EXACTLY their boundary edge, no interior overlap.
  disjoint → A and B share NO point at all, completely separate.

overlaps vs disjoint:
  overlaps → A and B share SOME interior area but neither fully contains the other.
  disjoint → A and B share NO point at all, completely separate.

county seat of vs within:
  county seat of does NOT imply within for a city and a university.
